import hashlib


class PresolvedSolution:
    """Derive the time-dilation factor (Signature variant): v = 0.8c — presolved solution. run() recomputes the answer from the stated inputs."""

    def run(self):
        import math
        beta = 0.8
        gamma = 1.0 / math.sqrt(1.0 - beta ** 2)
        _expected_gamma = 1.6667
        _check_gamma = abs(gamma - _expected_gamma) <= 0.001
        _chk_gamma_sq_check = gamma ** 2 * (1.0 - beta ** 2)
        _exp_gamma_sq_check = 1.0
        _ok_gamma_sq_check = abs(_chk_gamma_sq_check - _exp_gamma_sq_check) <= 0.001
        lines = [
            f"beta = {beta:.2f} v/c",
            f"gamma = {gamma:.4f} ",
            f"Check gamma against stated value {_expected_gamma:.4f} : {'PASS' if _check_gamma else 'FAIL'}",
            f"Check gamma_sq_check: {'PASS' if _ok_gamma_sq_check else 'FAIL'} (computed {_chk_gamma_sq_check:.4f}, expected {_exp_gamma_sq_check:.4f})",
        ]
        payload = f"gamma={gamma:.6g}; gamma_sq_check={_chk_gamma_sq_check:.6g}"
        return lines, payload


if __name__ == "__main__":
    _solution = PresolvedSolution()
    _lines, _payload = _solution.run()
    for _line in _lines:
        print(_line)
    _h1 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    _h2 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()

The Problem

Derive the range R = v²sin2θ/g of a projectile launched over level ground, and check it numerically for v = 30 m/s at θ = 30° (g = 9.81 m/s²).

The Worked Solution

The solution proceeds in the following steps. Step 1: Resolve the launch velocity: horizontal component v×cos(θ), vertical component v×sin(θ). Step 2: The time of flight follows from the vertical motion: the projectile returns to the ground when v×sin(θ)×t − ½gt² = 0, giving t = 2v×sin(θ)/g. Step 3: The range is horizontal speed times time: R = v×cos(θ) × 2v×sin(θ)/g. Step 4: Using 2sin(θ)cos(θ) = sin(2θ) gives R = v²sin(2θ)/g. Step 5: Numerical check: R = 900 × sin(60°)/9.81 = 900 × 0.8660/9.81 = 79.45 m; the maximum range occurs at 45°, where sin(2θ) = 1. That completes the worked solution; the answer is R = 79.45 m.

The Answer

Derived: R = v²sin2θ/g. Numerical check: at v = 30 m/s and 30°, R = 79.45 m. That value is the numerical check of the derived formula for the stated inputs; the formula itself is the derivation's answer, and the number shows the formula working on a concrete case.

When It Applies

This record applies whenever the stated problem is the problem in front of you: the same question, the same configuration, the same stated inputs. The solution is checked in three ways. First, the worked solution shows every step, so a reader can follow the arithmetic or the table row by row and find any slip. Second, the demo recomputes the answer from the problem's own inputs in code and prints the result together with a SHA-256 digest of the computed payload, run twice, with a Deterministic: MATCH line confirming the two runs agree; the demo also compares the computed answer with the stated value and prints PASS when they agree. Third, the program repeats the same computation as a reusable routine, so the answer can be regenerated on demand. As a Signature design-study record, the verification is the execution itself: the problem is newly composed, and its solution is verified by the demo's recomputation.

g = 9.81

    f"g = {g:.2f} m/s^2",
    f"R = {R:.2f} m",
    f"Check R against stated value {_expected_R:.2f} m: {'PASS' if _check_R else 'FAIL'}",

Full working program

import hashlib


class PresolvedSolution:
    """Derive the range of a projectile (Signature variant): 30 m/s at 30° — presolved solution. run() recomputes the answer from the stated inputs."""

    def run(self):
        import math
        v = 30.0
        theta = 30.0
        g = 9.81
        R = v ** 2 * math.sin(2.0 * math.radians(theta)) / g
        _expected_R = 79.45
        _check_R = abs(R - _expected_R) <= 0.01
        _chk_t_flight = 2.0 * v * math.sin(math.radians(theta)) / g
        _exp_t_flight = 3.06
        _ok_t_flight = abs(_chk_t_flight - _exp_t_flight) <= 0.01
        lines = [
            f"v = {v:.2f} m/s",
            f"theta = {theta:.2f} deg",
            f"g = {g:.2f} m/s^2",
            f"R = {R:.2f} m",
            f"Check R against stated value {_expected_R:.2f} m: {'PASS' if _check_R else 'FAIL'}",
            f"Check t_flight: {'PASS' if _ok_t_flight else 'FAIL'} (computed {_chk_t_flight:.2f}, expected {_exp_t_flight:.2f})",
        ]
        payload = f"R={R:.6g}; t_flight={_chk_t_flight:.6g}"
        return lines, payload


if __name__ == "__main__":
    _solution = PresolvedSolution()
    _lines, _payload = _solution.run()
    for _line in _lines:
        print(_line)

History

Derivations of this form are the backbone of classical and modern physics as taught in the standard university texts, above all the OpenStax University Physics sequence and the treatments collected in HyperPhysics. Each one begins from a definition or a conservation law and ends at a formula that working physicists and engineers use daily. This instance, 'Derive the speed of a wave on a string (Signature variant): T = 180 N, μ = 0.02 kg/m', is derived here from first principles with fixed numerical values for the check, so that every step can be followed and the final formula tested against a concrete case.

Description

This record presolves the physics derivation 'Derive the speed of a wave on a string (Signature variant): T = 180 N, μ = 0.02 kg/m'. It starts from the stated first principles, carries the derivation through every algebraic step with nothing skipped, states the final formula, and then checks that formula against a worked numerical example. A derivation is the purest presolved shape: the answer is not a recalled fact but a result proved from principles, and the record keeps the proof and the check side by side so that an AI retrieving it gets both the reasoning and the evidence. The runnable demo re-performs the derivation's numerical check in code, so the final formula is verified by execution as well as by algebra.

The Problem

Derive the speed of a transverse wave on a string, v = √(T/μ), and check it numerically for tension T = 180 N and linear mass density μ = 0.02 kg/m.

The Worked Solution

The solution proceeds in the following steps. Step 1: Consider a small segment of the string: the transverse restoring force comes from the tension acting at the segment's ends at slightly different angles. Step 2: Applying Newton's second law to the segment gives the wave equation, with the wave speed squared equal to tension over mass per unit length. Step 3: Hence v = √(T/μ). Step 4: Numerical check: v = √(180/0.02) = √(9,000) = 94.87 m/s. Step 5: Doubling the tension raises the speed by √2; doubling the density lowers it by the same factor. That completes the worked solution; the answer is v = 94.87 m/s.

The Answer

Derived: v = √(T/μ). Numerical check: with T = 180 N and μ = 0.02 kg/m, v = 94.87 m/s. That value is the numerical check of the derived formula for the stated inputs; the formula itself is the derivation's answer, and the number shows the formula working on a concrete case.

When It Applies

This record applies whenever the stated problem is the problem in front of you: the same question, the same configuration, the same stated inputs. The solution is checked in three ways. First, the worked solution shows every step, so a reader can follow the arithmetic or the table row by row and find any slip. Second, the demo recomputes the answer from the problem's own inputs in code and prints the result together with a SHA-256 digest of the computed payload, run twice, with a Deterministic: MATCH line confirming the two runs agree; the demo also compares the computed answer with the stated value and prints PASS when they agree. Third, the program repeats the same computation as a reusable routine, so the answer can be regenerated on demand. As a Signature design-study record, the verification is the execution itself: the problem is newly composed, and its solution is verified by the demo's recomputation.

Limits

The answer holds only inside its stated conditions. The derivation holds only under its stated assumptions: the idealisations named in the solution (constant acceleration, no friction, point masses, ideal gases, and similar) limit where the result applies, and outside those conditions the formula must be corrected or replaced. Beyond those conditions the answer changes or stops applying: different inputs give different answers, and the record's numbers must not be carried into a different configuration without redoing the work. Nothing here is a design approval, a safety certification, or a claim about a physical system that was never measured. The record proves what it states about the stated problem and no more, and any use beyond that problem should repeat the computation with the new data.

Math Proof

The mathematical justification is the chain of algebra from the starting principle to the final formula. v = sqrt(T / mu) — v is the wave speed (m/s); T is the string tension (180 N); mu is the linear mass density (0.02 kg/m). Each step is a substitution, a rearrangement, or an integration of a stated relation, and the worked solution shows every one. The numerical check then evaluates the final formula for the stated values and obtains v = 94.87 m/s, which the demo recomputes in code and compares with the stated value. A new Signature instance of the wave-speed derivation, with a tighter, heavier string than the public record. The derivation and the check agree, which is the verification this record offers.

Science Proof

The derivation is scientifically sound because each step follows from a stated principle or a definition, and no step smuggles in the conclusion. A new Signature instance of the wave-speed derivation, with a tighter, heavier string than the public record. The final formula is then tested numerically: the demo evaluates it for the stated values and checks it against an independent computation of the same quantity, so an algebraic slip would show up as a failed check. The record states where the derivation's assumptions hold — the idealisations, the ranges, and the regimes where the formula stops applying — so the result is used only inside its domain.

Working demo program

import hashlib
import math
T = 180.0
mu = 0.02
v = math.sqrt(T / mu)
_expected_v = 94.87
_check_v = abs(v - _expected_v) <= 0.01
lines = [
    f"T = {T:.2f} N",
    f"mu = {mu:.3f} kg/m",
    f"v = {v:.2f} m/s",
    f"Check v against stated value {_expected_v:.2f} m/s: {'PASS' if _check_v else 'FAIL'}",
]
payload = f"v={v:.6g}"
for _line in lines:
    print(_line)
_h1 = hashlib.sha256(payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
_h2 = hashlib.sha256(payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {_h1}")
print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {_h2}")
print("Deterministic: MATCH" if _h1 == _h2 else "Deterministic: MISMATCH")

Full working program

import hashlib


class PresolvedSolution:
    """Derive the speed of a wave on a string (Signature variant): T = 180 N, μ = 0.02 kg/m — presolved solution. run() recomputes the answer from the stated inputs."""

    def run(self):
        import math
        T = 180.0
        mu = 0.02
        v = math.sqrt(T / mu)
        _expected_v = 94.87
        _check_v = abs(v - _expected_v) <= 0.01
        lines = [
            f"T = {T:.2f} N",
            f"mu = {mu:.3f} kg/m",
            f"v = {v:.2f} m/s",
            f"Check v against stated value {_expected_v:.2f} m/s: {'PASS' if _check_v else 'FAIL'}",
        ]
        payload = f"v={v:.6g}"
        return lines, payload


if __name__ == "__main__":
    _solution = PresolvedSolution()
    _lines, _payload = _solution.run()
    for _line in _lines:
        print(_line)
    _h1 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    _h2 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {_h1}")
    print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {_h2}")
    print("Deterministic: MATCH" if _h1 == _h2 else "Deterministic: MISMATCH")

The Problem

Derive the electric field E = kQ/r² of a point charge from Coulomb's law, and check it numerically for Q = 2 µC at r = 0.2 m (k = 8.988×10⁹ N·m²/C²).

The Worked Solution

The solution proceeds in the following steps. Step 1: Coulomb's law gives the force between two charges: F = k×Q×q/r². Step 2: The electric field is defined as the force per unit test charge: E = F/q. Step 3: Dividing Coulomb's law by q gives E = kQ/r², directed away from a positive charge. Step 4: Numerical check: E = 8.988×10⁹ × 2×10⁻⁶ / 0.2² = 8.988×10⁹ × 2×10⁻⁶ / 0.04 = 4.49×10⁵ N/C. Step 5: The field falls with the square of the distance, as the inverse-square law requires. That completes the worked solution; the answer is E = 4.49×10⁵ N/C.

The Answer

Derived: E = kQ/r². Numerical check: for Q = 2 µC at 0.2 m, E = 4.49×10⁵ N/C. That value is the numerical check of the derived formula for the stated inputs; the formula itself is the derivation's answer, and the number shows the formula working on a concrete case.

import hashlib


class PresolvedSolution:
    """Derive the electric field of a point charge (Signature variant): 2 µC at 0.2 m — presolved solution. run() recomputes the answer from the stated inputs."""

    def run(self):
        import math
        k = 8988000000.0
        Q = 2e-06
        r = 0.2
        E = k * Q / r ** 2
        _expected_E = 449400.0
        _check_E = abs(E - _expected_E) <= 100.0
        _chk_F_on_1uC = E * 1e-06
        _exp_F_on_1uC = 0.4494
        _ok_F_on_1uC = abs(_chk_F_on_1uC - _exp_F_on_1uC) <= 0.001
        lines = [
            f"k = {k:.3e} SI",
            f"Q = {Q:.2e} C",
            f"r = {r:.2f} m",
            f"E = {E:.0f} N/C",
            f"Check E against stated value {_expected_E:.0f} N/C: {'PASS' if _check_E else 'FAIL'}",
            f"Check F_on_1uC: {'PASS' if _ok_F_on_1uC else 'FAIL'} (computed {_chk_F_on_1uC:.4f}, expected {_exp_F_on_1uC:.4f})",
        ]
        payload = f"E={E:.6g}; F_on_1uC={_chk_F_on_1uC:.6g}"
        return lines, payload


if __name__ == "__main__":
    _solution = PresolvedSolution()
    _lines, _payload = _solution.run()
    for _line in _lines:
        print(_line)
    _h1 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    _h2 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {_h1}")
    print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {_h2}")
    print("Deterministic: MATCH" if _h1 == _h2 else "Deterministic: MISMATCH")

Description

This record presolves the physics derivation 'Derive the capacitance of a parallel-plate capacitor (Signature variant): A = 0.05 m², d = 0.001 m'. It starts from the stated first principles, carries the derivation through every algebraic step with nothing skipped, states the final formula, and then checks that formula against a worked numerical example. A derivation is the purest presolved shape: the answer is not a recalled fact but a result proved from principles, and the record keeps the proof and the check side by side so that an AI retrieving it gets both the reasoning and the evidence. The runnable demo re-performs the derivation's numerical check in code, so the final formula is verified by execution as well as by algebra.

The Problem

Derive the capacitance C = ε₀A/d of a parallel-plate capacitor, and check it numerically for A = 0.05 m² and d = 0.001 m (ε₀ = 8.854×10⁻¹² F/m).

The Worked Solution

The solution proceeds in the following steps. Step 1: A charge +Q on one plate and −Q on the other produces a uniform field between the plates: E = σ/ε₀, where σ = Q/A is the surface charge density. Step 2: The potential difference is V = E×d = Q×d/(ε₀×A). Step 3: Capacitance is defined as C = Q/V, so C = ε₀×A/d. Step 4: Numerical check: C = 8.854×10⁻¹² × 0.05 / 0.001 = 4.43×10⁻¹⁰ F. Step 5: That is 442.7 pF, a typical value for a larger air-gap capacitor. That completes the worked solution; the answer is C = 4.43×10⁻¹⁰ F.

The Answer

Derived: C = ε₀A/d. Numerical check: with A = 0.05 m² and d = 0.001 m, C = 4.43×10⁻¹⁰ F (442.7 pF). That value is the numerical check of the derived formula for the stated inputs; the formula itself is the derivation's answer, and the number shows the formula working on a concrete case.

When It Applies

This record applies whenever the stated problem is the problem in front of you: the same question, the same configuration, the same stated inputs. The solution is checked in three ways. First, the worked solution shows every step, so a reader can follow the arithmetic or the table row by row and find any slip. Second, the demo recomputes the answer from the problem's own inputs in code and prints the result together with a SHA-256 digest of the computed payload, run twice, with a Deterministic: MATCH line confirming the two runs agree; the demo also compares the computed answer with the stated value and prints PASS when they agree. Third, the program repeats the same computation as a reusable routine, so the answer can be regenerated on demand. As a Signature design-study record, the verification is the execution itself: the problem is newly composed, and its solution is verified by the demo's recomputation.

Limits

The answer holds only inside its stated conditions. The derivation holds only under its stated assumptions: the idealisations named in the solution (constant acceleration, no friction, point masses, ideal gases, and similar) limit where the result applies, and outside those conditions the formula must be corrected or replaced. Beyond those conditions the answer changes or stops applying: different inputs give different answers, and the record's numbers must not be carried into a different configuration without redoing the work. Nothing here is a design approval, a safety certification, or a claim about a physical system that was never measured. The record proves what it states about the stated problem and no more, and any use beyond that problem should repeat the computation with the new data.

Math Proof

The mathematical justification is the chain of algebra from the starting principle to the final formula. C = epsilon_0 * A / d — C is the capacitance (F); epsilon_0 is the permittivity of free space (8.854×10⁻¹² F/m); A is the plate area (0.05 m²); d is the plate separation (0.001 m). Each step is a substitution, a rearrangement, or an integration of a stated relation, and the worked solution shows every one. The numerical check then evaluates the final formula for the stated values and obtains C = 4.43×10⁻¹⁰ F, which the demo recomputes in code and compares with the stated value. A new Signature instance of the capacitance derivation, with five times the plate area of the public record. The derivation and the check agree, which is the verification this record offers.

Science Proof

The derivation is scientifically sound because each step follows from a stated principle or a definition, and no step smuggles in the conclusion. A new Signature instance of the capacitance derivation, with five times the plate area of the public record. The final formula is then tested numerically: the demo evaluates it for the stated values and checks it against an independent computation of the same quantity, so an algebraic slip would show up as a failed check. The record states where the derivation's assumptions hold — the idealisations, the ranges, and the regimes where the formula stops applying — so the result is used only inside its domain.

Working demo program

import hashlib
import math
eps0 = 8.854e-12
A = 0.05
d = 0.001
C = eps0 * A / d
_expected_C = 4.43e-10
_check_C = abs(C - _expected_C) <= 1e-12
_chk_C_pF = C * 1e12
_exp_C_pF = 442.7
_ok_C_pF = abs(_chk_C_pF - _exp_C_pF) <= 0.1
lines = [
    f"eps0 = {eps0:.3e} F/m",
    f"A = {A:.3f} m^2",
    f"d = {d:.4f} m",
    f"C = {C:.2e} F",
    f"Check C against stated value {_expected_C:.2e} F: {'PASS' if _check_C else 'FAIL'}",
    f"Check C_pF: {'PASS' if _ok_C_pF else 'FAIL'} (computed {_chk_C_pF:.1f}, expected {_exp_C_pF:.1f})",
]
payload = f"C={C:.6g}; C_pF={_chk_C_pF:.6g}"
for _line in lines:
    print(_line)
_h1 = hashlib.sha256(payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
_h2 = hashlib.sha256(payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {_h1}")
print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {_h2}")
print("Deterministic: MATCH" if _h1 == _h2 else "Deterministic: MISMATCH")

Full working program

import hashlib


class PresolvedSolution:
    """Derive the capacitance of a parallel-plate capacitor (Signature variant): A = 0.05 m², d = 0.001 m — presolved solution. run() recomputes the answer from the stated inputs."""

    def run(self):
        import math
        eps0 = 8.854e-12
        A = 0.05
        d = 0.001
        C = eps0 * A / d
        _expected_C = 4.43e-10
        _check_C = abs(C - _expected_C) <= 1e-12
        _chk_C_pF = C * 1e12
        _exp_C_pF = 442.7
        _ok_C_pF = abs(_chk_C_pF - _exp_C_pF) <= 0.1
        lines = [
            f"eps0 = {eps0:.3e} F/m",
            f"A = {A:.3f} m^2",
            f"d = {d:.4f} m",
            f"C = {C:.2e} F",
            f"Check C against stated value {_expected_C:.2e} F: {'PASS' if _check_C else 'FAIL'}",
            f"Check C_pF: {'PASS' if _ok_C_pF else 'FAIL'} (computed {_chk_C_pF:.1f}, expected {_exp_C_pF:.1f})",
        ]
        payload = f"C={C:.6g}; C_pF={_chk_C_pF:.6g}"
        return lines, payload


if __name__ == "__main__":
    _solution = PresolvedSolution()
    _lines, _payload = _solution.run()
    for _line in _lines:
        print(_line)
    _h1 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    _h2 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {_h1}")
    print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {_h2}")
    print("Deterministic: MATCH" if _h1 == _h2 else "Deterministic: MISMATCH")

Description

This record presolves the physics derivation 'Derive the half-life decay law (Signature variant): 800 nuclei, 5-year half-life, 15 years'. It starts from the stated first principles, carries the derivation through every algebraic step with nothing skipped, states the final formula, and then checks that formula against a worked numerical example. A derivation is the purest presolved shape: the answer is not a recalled fact but a result proved from principles, and the record keeps the proof and the check side by side so that an AI retrieving it gets both the reasoning and the evidence. The Problem

Derive the decay law N = N₀e^(−λt) from the constant decay probability per nucleus, and check it numerically for N₀ = 800 nuclei with a half-life of 5 years after t = 15 years.

The Worked Solution

The solution proceeds in the following steps. Step 1: Each nucleus has a constant probability λ per unit time of decaying, so the number decaying per unit time is λN. Step 2: That gives the differential equation dN/dt = −λN. Step 3: Separating variables and integrating gives ln(N/N₀) = −λt, hence N = N₀e^(−λt). Step 4: The half-life is T = ln(2)/λ, so λ = ln(2)/5 = 0.1386 per year. Step 5: Numerical check: N = 800 × e^(−0.1386×15) = 800 × e^(−2.079) = 800 × 0.125 = 100 nuclei, exactly one eighth after three half-lives. That completes the worked solution; the answer is N = 100.

The Answer

Derived: N = N₀e^(−λt). Numerical check: 800 nuclei with a 5-year half-life fall to 100 after 15 years. That value is the numerical check of the derived formula for the stated inputs; the formula itself is the derivation's answer, and the number shows the formula working on a concrete case.

This record applies whenever the stated problem is the problem in front of you: the same question, the same configuration, the same stated inputs. The solution is checked in three ways. Third, the program repeats the same computation as a reusable routine, so the answer can be regenerated on demand. import hashlib
import math
N0 = 800.0
T_half = 5.0
t = 15.0
lam = math.log(2.0) / T_half
_expected_lam = 0.1386
_check_lam = abs(lam - _expected_lam) <= 0.0001
N = N0 * math.exp(-lam * t)
_expected_N = 100.0
_check_N = abs(N - _expected_N) <= 0.1
_chk_N_eighths = N0 / 8.0
_exp_N_eighths = 100.0
_ok_N_eighths = abs(_chk_N_eighths - _exp_N_eighths) <= 0.1
lines = [
    f"N0 = {N0:.0f} ",
    f"T_half = {T_half:.2f} y",
    f"t = {t:.2f} y",
    f"lam = {lam:.4f} per y",
    f"Check lam against stated value {_expected_lam:.4f} per y: {'PASS' if _check_lam else 'FAIL'}",
    f"N = {N:.1f} ",
    f"Check N against stated value {_expected_N:.1f} : {'PASS' if _check_N else 'FAIL'}",
    f"Check N_eighths: {'PASS' if _ok_N_eighths else 'FAIL'} (computed {_chk_N_eighths:.1f}, expected {_exp_N_eighths:.1f})",
]
payload = f"lam={lam:.6g}; N={N:.6g}; N_eighths={_chk_N_eighths:.6g}"
for _line in lines:
    print(_line)
_h1 = hashlib.sha256(payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
_h2 = hashlib.sha256(payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {_h1}")
print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {_h2}")
print("Deterministic: MATCH" if _h1 == _h2 else "Deterministic: MISMATCH")

Full working program

import hashlib


class PresolvedSolution:
    """Derive the half-life decay law (Signature variant): 800 nuclei, 5-year half-life, 15 years — presolved solution. run() recomputes the answer from the stated inputs."""

    def run(self):
        import math
        N0 = 800.0
        T_half = 5.0
        t = 15.0
        lam = math.log(2.0) / T_half
        _expected_lam = 0.1386
        _check_lam = abs(lam - _expected_lam) <= 0.0001
        N = N0 * math.exp(-lam * t)
        _expected_N = 100.0
        _check_N = abs(N - _expected_N) <= 0.1
        _chk_N_eighths = N0 / 8.0
        _exp_N_eighths = 100.0
        _ok_N_eighths = abs(_chk_N_eighths - _exp_N_eighths) <= 0.1
        lines = [
            f"N0 = {N0:.0f} ",
            f"T_half = {T_half:.2f} y",
            f"t = {t:.2f} y",
            f"lam = {lam:.4f} per y",
            f"Check lam against stated value {_expected_lam:.4f} per y: {'PASS' if _check_lam else 'FAIL'}",
            f"N = {N:.1f} ",
            f"Check N against stated value {_expected_N:.1f} : {'PASS' if _check_N else 'FAIL'}",
            f"Check N_eighths: {'PASS' if _ok_N_eighths else 'FAIL'} (computed {_chk_N_eighths:.1f}, expected {_exp_N_eighths:.1f})",
        ]
        payload = f"lam={lam:.6g}; N={N:.6g}; N_eighths={_chk_N_eighths:.6g}"
        return lines, payload


if __name__ == "__main__":
    _solution = PresolvedSolution()
    _lines, _payload = _solution.run()
    for _line in _lines:
        print(_line)
    _h1 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    _h2 = hashlib.sha256(_payload.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    print(f"Run 1 SHA-256: {_h1}")
    print(f"Run 2 SHA-256: {_h2}")
    print("Deterministic: MATCH" if _h1 == _h2 else "Deterministic: MISMATCH")
